**Elon Reeve Musk** ([/ˈiːlɒn/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/0/0f/Elon_name_pronunciation.wav/Elon_name_pronunciation.wav.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/0/0f/Elon_name_pronunciation.wav/Elon_name_pronunciation.wav.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup> [*EE-lon*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Pronunciation_respelling_key); born June 28, 1971) is a businessman, industrialist, and former public official who is the [chief executive officer](52234.md) (CEO) and largest shareholder of [Tesla](5533631.md) and [SpaceX](832774.md). Musk has been the [wealthiest person in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_World's_Billionaires) since 2025, and briefly became the only [trillionaire](47418795.md) (in terms of [US dollars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_dollars)) in June 2026; as of September 2026, *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* estimates [his net worth](76211626.md) to be US\$964 billion.

Born into the wealthy [Musk family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musk_family) in [Pretoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pretoria), South Africa, Musk emigrated in 1989 to Canada; he has Canadian citizenship since his mother was born there. He received bachelor's degrees in 1997 from the [University of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Pennsylvania) before moving to [California](5407.md) to pursue business ventures. In 1995, Musk co-founded [Zip2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zip2), a web software company. Following its sale in 1999, he co-founded [X.com](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X.com_\(bank\)), an [e-commerce payment system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E-commerce_payment_system) that merged with [Confinity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confinity) in March 2000 to form [PayPal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PayPal), which was acquired by [eBay](130495.md) in 2002. Musk also became an American citizen in 2002.

In 2002, Musk founded and became CEO and chief engineer of SpaceX, a [space technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_technology) company; the company has since led innovations in [reusable rockets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SpaceX_reusable_launch_system_development_program) and [commercial spaceflight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commercial_spaceflight). Musk joined Tesla as an early investor in 2004 and became its CEO and product architect in 2008; it has since become a leader in [electric vehicles](279350.md). In 2015, Musk co-founded [OpenAI](48795986.md) to advance [artificial intelligence](1164.md) (AI) research, but later left; his growing discontent with the organization's direction and leadership in the [AI boom](73291899.md) in the 2020s led him to establish [xAI](74330685.md), which became a subsidiary of SpaceX in 2026. In 2022, [he acquired Twitter](70614210.md), a [social networking service](2041117.md), and [made significant changes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter_under_Elon_Musk), including rebranding it as [X](9988187.md) in 2023. His other businesses include [Neuralink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuralink), a [neurotechnology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neurotechnology) company that he co-founded in 2016, and [the Boring Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Boring_Company), a tunneling company that he founded in 2017. In November 2025, Tesla approved a pay package worth \$1 trillion for Musk, which he is to receive over 10 years if certain milestones are met, such as achieving a [market capitalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_capitalization) of \$8.5 trillion. Musk became the first US-dollar trillionaire upon the [initial public offering of SpaceX](82660746.md) in June 2026, though a SpaceX stock collapse brought him under the threshold by the next month.

Musk was the largest donor in the [2024 U.S. presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_U.S._presidential_election), where he supported [Donald Trump](4848272.md). After Trump was inaugurated in January 2025, Musk was [Senior Advisor to the President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senior_Advisor_to_the_President) and the *[de facto](43594.md)* head of the [Department of Government Efficiency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Government_Efficiency) (DOGE). Musk left the Trump administration in May 2025 and returned to managing his companies; shortly thereafter he had [a public feud with Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump%E2%80%93Musk_feud).&#x20;

Musk is a supporter of global [far-right](221220.md) politics, figures, and political parties. [His political activities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_activities_of_Elon_Musk), statements and [views](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Views_of_Elon_Musk) have made him [a polarizing figure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_image_of_Elon_Musk). He has been criticized for making unscientific and misleading statements, including spreading [COVID-19 misinformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Views_of_Elon_Musk#COVID-19_pandemic), [promoting conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Views_of_Elon_Musk#Conspiracy_theories), and [affirming antisemitic, white nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Views_of_Elon_Musk#Race_and_white_nationalism), racist, and [transphobic comments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Views_of_Elon_Musk#On_transgender_topics). His acquisition of Twitter was controversial because, following his pledge to decrease [censorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Censorship), there was [an increase in hate speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter_under_Elon_Musk#Increase_in_hate_speech) and [misinformation on the service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter_under_Elon_Musk#Handling_of_misinformation_and_disinformation). His role in the [second Trump administration](78298108.md) attracted [public backlash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protests_against_Elon_Musk), particularly in [response to DOGE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Response_to_the_Department_of_Government_Efficiency) and its [cuts to the US Agency for International Development](82201872.md) (USAID).

## Early life and education

See also: [Musk family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musk_family)

Elon Reeve Musk was born on June 28, 1971, in [Pretoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pretoria), South Africa's administrative capital.[^cite_note-Eligon-2022-5][^cite_note-6] He is of British and [Pennsylvania Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Dutch) ancestry.[^cite_note-sjmn20140411-7][^cite_note-8] His mother, [Maye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maye_Musk) ([née](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birth_name#Maiden_and_married_names) Haldeman), is a model and dietitian born in [Saskatchewan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saskatchewan), Canada, and raised in South Africa.[^cite_note-9][^cite_note-10][^cite_note-Usborne-2018-11] Musk therefore holds both South African and Canadian citizenship from birth.[^cite_note-12] His father, [Errol Musk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Errol_Musk), is a South African [electromechanical engineer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electromechanics), pilot, sailor, consultant, [emerald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emerald) dealer, and property developer, who partly owned a rental lodge at [Timbavati Private Nature Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timbavati_Private_Nature_Reserve).[^cite_note-Isaacson-13][^cite_note-14][^cite_note-NewYorker2009-15][^cite_note-Smith-16]

His maternal grandfather, [Joshua N. Haldeman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joshua_N._Haldeman), who died in a plane crash when Musk was a toddler, was an American-born Canadian chiropractor, aviator and political activist in the [technocracy movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technocracy_movement)[^cite_note-17][^cite_note-18] who moved to South Africa in 1950.[^cite_note-McGreal-19] Haldeman's anti-government, anti-democratic and conspiracist views, which included the promotion of far-right antisemitic conspiracy theories,[^cite_note-20][^cite_note-Benton2-21] "fanatical" support of [apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apartheid),[^cite_note-Benton2-21] and according to Errol, support of [Nazism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism),[^cite_note-McGreal-19] have been suggested as an [influence on Musk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joshua_N._Haldeman#Influence_on_Elon_Musk).[^cite_note-22][^cite_note-23][^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25] During his childhood, Musk was told stories by his grandmother of Haldeman's travels and exploits, and he has suggested that all of Haldeman's descendants have his "desire for adventure, exploration – doing crazy things".[^cite_note-26]

Musk has a younger brother, [Kimbal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimbal_Musk), a younger sister, [Tosca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tosca_Musk), and four paternal half-siblings.[^cite_note-27][^cite_note-28][^cite_note-Usborne-2018-11][^cite_note-29] He was [baptized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptism) as a child in the [Anglican Church of Southern Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglican_Church_of_Southern_Africa).[^cite_note-Higgins2024-30][^cite_note-Rees2024-31] The Musk family was wealthy during his youth.[^cite_note-Smith-16] Despite both Musk and Errol previously stating that Errol was a part owner of a Zambian emerald mine,[^cite_note-Smith-16] in 2023, Errol recounted that the deal he made was to receive "a portion of the emeralds produced at three small mines".[^cite_note-32][^cite_note-33] Errol was elected to the Pretoria City Council as a representative of the anti-apartheid [Progressive Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive_Party_\(South_Africa\)) and has said that his children shared their father's dislike of [apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apartheid).[^cite_note-Eligon-2022-5]

After his parents divorced in 1979, Musk, aged around 9, chose to live with his father because he had an *[Encyclopædia Britannica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encyclop%C3%A6dia_Britannica)* set and a computer.[^cite_note-34][^cite_note-sjmn20140411-7][^cite_note-Isaacson-13] He later regretted his decision and became estranged from his father.[^cite_note-rollingstone20171115-35] Musk has recounted trips to a wilderness school that he described as a "paramilitary *[Lord of the Flies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lord_of_the_Flies)*" where "bullying was a virtue" and children were encouraged to fight over rations.[^cite_note-36] In one incident, after an altercation with a fellow pupil, Musk was thrown down concrete steps and beaten severely, leading to him being hospitalized for his injuries.[^cite_note-Isaacson23-37] He described his father berating him after he was discharged from the hospital.[^cite_note-Isaacson23-37] Errol denied berating Musk and claimed, "The \[other] boy had just lost his father to suicide, and Musk had called him stupid. He had a tendency to call people stupid. How could I possibly blame that child?"[^cite_note-Bhaimiya-38]

Musk was an enthusiastic reader of books, and had attributed his success in part to having read *[The Lord of the Rings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Lord_of_the_Rings)*, the [*Foundation* series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foundation_\(book_series\)), and [*The Hitchhiker's Guide to the Galaxy*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hitchhiker's_Guide_to_the_Galaxy_\(novel\)).[^cite_note-NewYorker2009-15][^cite_note-39] At age ten, he developed an interest in computing and video games, teaching himself how to program from the [VIC-20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VIC-20) user manual.[^cite_note-Vance-40] At age twelve, in 1983, Musk sold his [BASIC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BASIC)-based game *Blastar* to *PC and Office Technology* magazine for approximately \$500 (equivalent to \$1,600 in 2025).[^cite_note-play-41][^cite_note-42]

### Education

An ornate school building

Musk graduated from [Pretoria Boys High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pretoria_Boys_High_School) in [Pretoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pretoria), South Africa.

Musk attended [Waterkloof House Preparatory School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterkloof_House_Preparatory_School), [Bryanston High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bryanston_High_School), and then [Pretoria Boys High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pretoria_Boys_High_School), where he graduated.[^cite_note-43] He was a decent but unexceptional student, earning a 61/100 in [Afrikaans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afrikaans) and a B on his senior math certification.[^cite_note-44] He applied for a Canadian passport [through](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jus_sanguinis) his Canadian-born mother to avoid South Africa's [mandatory military service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conscription_in_South_Africa),[^cite_note-arrived-45][^cite_note-46] which would have forced him to participate in the apartheid regime's mandatory military service for young white South African men,[^cite_note-Eligon-2022-5] as well as to ease his path to immigration to the United States.[^cite_note-Esquire-47] While waiting for his application to be processed, he attended the [University of Pretoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Pretoria) for five months.[^cite_note-48]

Musk arrived in Canada in June 1989, connected with a second cousin in [Saskatchewan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saskatchewan),[^cite_note-49][^cite_note-50] and worked odd jobs, including at a farm and a lumber mill.[^cite_note-51] In 1990, he entered [Queen's University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen's_University_at_Kingston) in [Kingston, Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingston,_Ontario).[^cite_note-52][^cite_note-53] Two years later, he transferred to the [University of Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Pennsylvania), where he studied until 1995.[^cite_note-ii-54] Although Musk has said that he earned his degrees in 1995, the University of Pennsylvania did not award them until 1997 – a [Bachelor of Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachelor_of_Arts) in physics and a [Bachelor of Science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachelor_of_Science) in economics from the university's [Wharton School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wharton_School).[^cite_note-Vance368-55][^cite_note-56][^cite_note-VanceA1-57][^cite_note-58][^cite_note-59] He reportedly hosted large, ticketed house parties to help pay for tuition, and wrote a business plan for an electronic book-scanning service similar to [Google Books](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Google_Books).[^cite_note-ironman-60]

In 1994, Musk held two internships in [Silicon Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Valley): one at energy storage startup Pinnacle Research Institute, which investigated electrolytic [supercapacitors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supercapacitor) for energy storage, and another at [Palo Alto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palo_Alto,_California)–based startup [Rocket Science Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocket_Science_Games).[^cite_note-61][^cite_note-62] In 1995, he was accepted to a graduate program in [materials science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Materials_science) at [Stanford University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanford_University), but did not enroll.[^cite_note-VanceA1-57][^cite_note-Vance368-55][^cite_note-Sacchetti1026-63] Musk decided to join the [Internet boom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dot-com_bubble) of the 1990s, applying for a job at [Netscape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netscape), to which he reportedly never received a response.[^cite_note-64][^cite_note-arrived-45] *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* reported that Musk lacked legal authorization to remain and work in the United States after failing to enroll at Stanford.[^cite_note-Sacchetti1026-63] In response, Musk said he was allowed to work at that time and that his student visa transitioned to an [H1-B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H-1B_visa). According to numerous former business associates and shareholders, Musk said he was on a student visa at the time.[^cite_note-65]

## Business career

Main article: [Business career of Elon Musk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_career_of_Elon_Musk)

### Zip2

Main article: [Zip2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zip2)

| External videos |
| --- |
| video icon [Musk speaks of his early business experience during a 2014 commencement speech at University of Southern California](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=e7Qh-vwpYH8) on YouTube |

In 1995, Musk, his brother [Kimbal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimbal), and Greg Kouri founded [Zip2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zip2), a web software company, with funding from a group of angel investors.[^cite_note-66] They housed the venture at a small rented office in Palo Alto.[^cite_note-shower-67] Replying to *[Rolling Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone)*, Musk denounced the notion that they started their company with funds borrowed from Musk's father Errol Musk,[^cite_note-rollingstone20171115-35] but in a tweet, he recognized that his father contributed 10% of a later funding round.[^cite_note-twitterzip2-68] The company developed and marketed an Internet city guide for the newspaper publishing industry, with maps, directions, and yellow pages.[^cite_note-69]

According to Musk, "The website was up during the day and I was coding it at night, seven days a week, all the time."[^cite_note-shower-67] To impress investors, Musk built a large plastic structure around a standard computer to create the impression that Zip2 was powered by a small supercomputer.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVance201764-70] The Musk brothers obtained contracts with *[The New York Times](30680.md)* and the *[Chicago Tribune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Tribune),*[^cite_note-71] and persuaded the board of directors to abandon plans for a merger with [CitySearch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CitySearch).[^cite_note-72] Musk's attempts to become CEO were thwarted by the board.[^cite_note-73] [Compaq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compaq) acquired Zip2 for \$307 million in cash in February 1999 (equivalent to \$590,000,000 in 2025),[^cite_note-74][^cite_note-75] and Musk received \$22 million (equivalent to \$43,000,000 in 2025) for his 7-percent share.[^cite_note-Vance109-76]

### X.com and PayPal

Main articles: [X.com (bank)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X.com_\(bank\)), [PayPal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PayPal), and [PayPal Mafia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PayPal_Mafia)

In 1999, Musk co-founded X.com, an online financial services and e-mail payment company.[^cite_note-77] The startup was one of the first [federally insured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Deposit_Insurance_Corporation) online banks, and, in its initial months of operation, over 200,000 customers joined the service.[^cite_note-78] The company's investors regarded Musk as inexperienced and replaced him with [Intuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intuit) CEO Bill Harris by the end of the year.[^cite_note-vance86-79] The following year, X.com merged with online bank [Confinity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confinity) to avoid competition.[^cite_note-shower-67][^cite_note-vance86-79][^cite_note-80] Founded by [Max Levchin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Levchin) and [Peter Thiel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Thiel),[^cite_note-81] Confinity had its own money-transfer service, [PayPal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PayPal), which was more popular than X.com's service.[^cite_note-82]

Within the merged company, Musk returned as CEO. Musk's preference for [Microsoft](19001.md) software over [Unix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unix) created a rift in the company and caused Thiel to resign.[^cite_note-83] Due to resulting technological issues and lack of a cohesive business model, the board ousted Musk and replaced him with Thiel in 2000.[^cite_note-84][^cite_note-87] Under Thiel, the company focused on the PayPal service and was renamed PayPal in 2001.[^cite_note-vance89-86][^cite_note-88] In 2002, PayPal was acquired by [eBay](130495.md) for \$1.5 billion (equivalent to \$2.7×10<sup>9</sup> in 2025) in stock, of which Musk—the largest shareholder with 11.72% of shares—received \$175.8 million (equivalent to \$320,000,000 in 2025).[^cite_note-89][^cite_note-90] In 2017, Musk purchased the domain X.com from PayPal for an undisclosed amount, stating that it had sentimental value.[^cite_note-91][^cite_note-92] He later used the domain in his rebrand of Twitter to X.

### SpaceX

Main article: [SpaceX](832774.md)



Musk explaining [Starship](59118602.md) capabilities to leaders of [North American Aerospace Defense Command](42905.md), U.S. Northern Command, and [Air Force Space Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Operations_Command) in 2019

In 2001, Musk became involved with the nonprofit [Mars Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mars_Society) and discussed funding plans to place a [growth-chamber for plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_farming) on [Mars](14640471.md).[^cite_note-93] Seeking a way to launch the greenhouse payloads into space, Musk made two unsuccessful trips to Moscow to purchase [intercontinental ballistic missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intercontinental_ballistic_missiles) (ICBMs) from Russian companies [NPO Lavochkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NPO_Lavochkin) and [Kosmotras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosmotras). Musk instead decided to start a company to build affordable rockets.[^cite_note-Vance-2015-94] With \$100 million of his early fortune,[^cite_note-95] (equivalent to \$180,000,000 in 2025) Musk founded SpaceX in May 2002 and became the company's CEO and Chief Engineer.[^cite_note-96][^cite_note-97]

SpaceX attempted its first launch of the [Falcon 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falcon_1) rocket in 2006.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBerger2021178–182-98] Although the rocket failed to reach [Earth orbit](568972.md), it was awarded a [Commercial Orbital Transportation Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commercial_Orbital_Transportation_Services) program contract from [NASA](18426568.md), then led by [Mike Griffin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_D._Griffin).[^cite_note-99][^cite_note-100] After two more failed attempts that nearly caused Musk to go bankrupt,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBerger2021178–182-98] SpaceX succeeded in launching the Falcon 1 into orbit in 2008.[^cite_note-101] Later that year, SpaceX received a \$1.6 billion NASA contract (equivalent to \$2.4×10<sup>9</sup> in 2025) for [Falcon 9](2647515.md)-launched [Dragon spacecraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SpaceX_Dragon) flights to the [International Space Station](15043.md) (ISS), replacing the [Space Shuttle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Shuttle) after its 2011 [retirement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Shuttle_retirement).[^cite_note-102] In 2012, the Dragon vehicle docked [with the ISS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SpaceX_COTS_Demo_Flight_2), a first for a commercial spacecraft.[^cite_note-103]

Working towards its goal of [reusable rockets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SpaceX_reusable_launch_system_development_program), in 2015 SpaceX successfully landed the [first stage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multistage_rocket) of a Falcon 9 on a land platform.[^cite_note-104] Later landings were achieved on [autonomous spaceport drone ships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_spaceport_drone_ship), an ocean-based recovery platform.[^cite_note-105] In 2018, SpaceX launched the [Falcon Heavy](31406060.md); the [inaugural mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falcon_Heavy_test_flight) carried [Musk's personal Tesla Roadster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elon_Musk's_Tesla_Roadster) as a [dummy payload](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boilerplate_\(spaceflight\)).[^cite_note-106][^cite_note-107] Since 2019,[^cite_note-108] SpaceX has been developing [Starship](59118602.md), a reusable, [super heavy-lift launch vehicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_heavy-lift_launch_vehicle) intended to replace the Falcon 9 and Falcon Heavy.[^cite_note-Roulette-2022-109] In 2020, SpaceX launched its first crewed flight, the [Demo-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crew_Dragon_Demo-2), becoming the first private company to place astronauts into orbit and dock a crewed spacecraft with the ISS.[^cite_note-110] In 2024, NASA awarded SpaceX an \$843 million (equivalent to \$865,000,000 in 2025) contract to build a spacecraft that NASA will use to [deorbit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atmospheric_entry) the ISS at the end of its lifespan.[^cite_note-111]

#### Starlink

Main article: [Starlink](45111627.md)

See also: [Starlink in the Russo-Ukrainian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starlink_in_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War)



50 Starlink satellites shortly before deployment to low Earth orbit, 2019

In 2015, SpaceX began development of the Starlink constellation of [low Earth orbit](47568.md) satellites to provide [satellite Internet access](769088.md).[^cite_note-112] After the launch of prototype satellites in 2018, the first large constellation was deployed in May 2019.[^cite_note-NYT-20190601-113] As of May 2025, over 7,600 Starlink satellites are operational,[^cite_note-sn2674441-114] comprising 65% of all operational Earth satellites.[^cite_note-d933-115] The total cost of the decade-long project to design, build, and deploy the constellation was estimated by SpaceX in 2020 to be \$10 billion (equivalent to \$1.2×10<sup>10</sup> in 2025).[^cite_note-116][^cite_note-119]

During the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine), Musk provided free Starlink service to Ukraine, permitting Internet access and communication at a yearly cost to SpaceX of \$400 million (equivalent to \$440,000,000 in 2025).[^cite_note-120][^cite_note-CNN_2022-10-13-121][^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123][^cite_note-124] However, Musk refused to block [Russian state media](15861222.md) on Starlink.[^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126] In 2023, Musk denied Ukraine's request to activate Starlink over [Crimea](163045.md) to aid an attack against the Russian navy, citing fears of a nuclear response.[^cite_note-Copp_2023_b431-127][^cite_note-128][^cite_note-129]

### Tesla

Main article: [Tesla, Inc.](5533631.md)

Musks stands, arms crossed and grinning, before a Tesla Model S

Musk next to a [Tesla Model S](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Model_S), 2011

Tesla, Inc., originally Tesla Motors, was incorporated in July 2003 by [Martin Eberhard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Eberhard) and [Marc Tarpenning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc_Tarpenning). Both men played active roles in the company's early development prior to Musk's involvement.[^cite_note-timelinefacts-130] Musk led the [Series A round](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Series_A_round) of investment in February 2004; he invested \$6.35 million (equivalent to \$11,000,000 in 2025), became the majority shareholder, and joined Tesla's board of directors as chairman.[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132] Musk took an active role within the company and oversaw [Roadster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Roadster_\(first_generation\)) product design, but was not deeply involved in day-to-day business operations.[^cite_note-133] Following a series of escalating conflicts in 2007 and the [2008 financial crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_financial_crisis), Eberhard was ousted from the firm.[^cite_note-134][^cite_note-135] Musk assumed leadership of the company as CEO and product architect in 2008.[^cite_note-136] A 2009 lawsuit settlement with Eberhard designated Musk as a Tesla co-founder, along with Tarpenning and two others.[^cite_note-CNET-137][^cite_note-FastCompany-138]

Tesla began delivery of the Roadster, an electric sports car, in 2008. With sales of about 2,500 vehicles, it was the first [mass production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_production) all-electric car to use [lithium-ion battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithium-ion_battery) cells.[^cite_note-139] Under Musk, Tesla has since launched several well-selling electric vehicles, including the four-door sedan [Model S](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Model_S) (2012), the crossover [Model X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Model_X) (2015), the mass-market sedan [Model 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Model_3) (2017), the crossover [Model Y](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Model_Y) (2020), and the pickup truck [Cybertruck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Cybertruck) (2023).[^cite_note-140][^cite_note-141][^cite_note-142][^cite_note-143][^cite_note-Krisher_2023-144] The company has also constructed multiple lithium-ion battery and electric vehicle factories, called [Gigafactories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory).[^cite_note-145]

In May 2020, Musk resigned as chairman of the board as part of the settlement of a [lawsuit from the SEC](#Legal_affairs) over him tweeting that funding had been "secured" for potentially taking Tesla private.[^cite_note-Goldstein_27-9-18-146][^cite_note-ct-147]

Since its [initial public offering](63879.md) in 2010,[^cite_note-148] Tesla stock has risen significantly; it became the most valuable carmaker in summer 2020,[^cite_note-149][^cite_note-150] and it entered the [S\&P 500](170992.md) later that year.[^cite_note-151][^cite_note-152] In October 2021, it reached a market capitalization of \$1 trillion (equivalent to \$1.2×10<sup>12</sup> in 2025), the sixth company in U.S. history to do so.[^cite_note-153]

#### SolarCity and Tesla Energy

Main articles: [SolarCity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SolarCity) and [Tesla Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla_Energy)

Musk provided the initial concept and financial capital for SolarCity, which his cousins [Lyndon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyndon_Rive) and Peter Rive founded in 2006.[^cite_note-154] By 2013, SolarCity was the second largest provider of solar power systems in the United States.[^cite_note-155] In 2014, Musk promoted the idea of SolarCity building an advanced production facility in [Buffalo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buffalo,_New_York), New York, triple the size of the largest solar plant in the United States.[^cite_note-156] Construction of [the factory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigafactory_New_York) started in 2014 and was completed in 2017. It operated as a joint venture with [Panasonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panasonic) until early 2020.[^cite_note-157][^cite_note-158]

Tesla acquired SolarCity for \$2 billion in 2016 (equivalent to \$2.7×10<sup>9</sup> in 2025) and merged it with its battery unit to create Tesla Energy. The deal's announcement resulted in a more than 10% drop in Tesla's stock price; at the time, SolarCity was facing liquidity issues.[^cite_note-ws-159] Multiple shareholder groups filed a lawsuit against Musk and Tesla's directors, stating that the purchase of SolarCity was done solely to benefit Musk and came at the expense of Tesla and its shareholders.[^cite_note-160][^cite_note-161] Tesla directors settled the lawsuit in January 2020, leaving Musk the sole remaining defendant.[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-163] Two years later, the court ruled in Musk's favor.[^cite_note-ws-159]

### Neuralink

Main article: [Neuralink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuralink)

Musk standing next to bulky medical equipment on a stage

Musk discussing a Neuralink device during a live demonstration in 2020

In 2016, Musk co-founded Neuralink, a [neurotechnology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neurotechnology) startup, with an investment of \$100 million.[^cite_note-sew-164][^cite_note-neuraverge-165] Neuralink aims to integrate the human brain with [artificial intelligence](1164.md) (AI) by creating devices that are embedded in the brain. Such technology could enhance memory or allow the devices to communicate with software.[^cite_note-neuraverge-165][^cite_note-166] The company also hopes to develop devices to treat neurological conditions like [spinal cord injuries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spinal_cord_injury).[^cite_note-pig-167] In 2022, Neuralink announced that clinical trials would begin by the end of the year.[^cite_note-168] In September 2023, the [Food and Drug Administration](11632.md) approved Neuralink to initiate six-year human trials.[^cite_note-169]

Neuralink has conducted [animal testing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animal_testing) on [macaques](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macaque) at the [University of California, Davis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_Davis). In 2021, the company released a video in which a macaque played the video game *[Pong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pong)* via a Neuralink implant. The company's animal trials—which have caused the deaths of some monkeys—have led to claims of [animal cruelty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cruelty_to_animals). The [Physicians Committee for Responsible Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physicians_Committee_for_Responsible_Medicine) has alleged that Neuralink violated the [Animal Welfare Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animal_Welfare_Act_of_1966).[^cite_note-170] Employees have complained that pressure from Musk to accelerate development has led to botched experiments and unnecessary animal deaths. In 2022, a federal probe was launched into possible animal welfare violations by Neuralink.[^cite_note-botched_monkeys-171]

### The Boring Company

Main article: [The Boring Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Boring_Company)

Musk speaks to a crowd of journalists. Behind him is a lighted tunnel.

Musk during the 2018 inauguration of the [Boring test tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boring_test_tunnel) in [Hawthorne, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawthorne,_California)

In 2017, Musk founded the Boring Company to construct tunnels; he also revealed plans for specialized, underground, high-occupancy vehicles that could travel up to 150 miles per hour (240 km/h) and thus circumvent above-ground traffic in major cities.[^cite_note-172][^cite_note-cnndogecoin-173] Early in 2017, the company began discussions with regulatory bodies and initiated construction of a 30-foot (9.1 m) wide, 50-foot (15 m) long, and 15-foot (4.6 m) deep "test trench" on the premises of SpaceX's offices, as that required no permits.[^cite_note-boring-174]

The [Los Angeles tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boring_test_tunnel), less than two miles (3.2 km) in length, debuted to journalists in 2018. It used Tesla Model Xs and was reported to be a rough ride while traveling at suboptimal speeds.[^cite_note-175] Two tunnel projects announced in 2018, in Chicago and West Los Angeles, have been canceled.[^cite_note-176][^cite_note-177] A tunnel beneath the [Las Vegas Convention Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Vegas_Convention_Center) was completed in early 2021.[^cite_note-178] Local officials have approved further expansions of the tunnel system.[^cite_note-179]

### X Corp.&#x20;

Main articles: [Acquisition of Twitter by Elon Musk](70614210.md) and [Twitter under Elon Musk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter_under_Elon_Musk)

| Avatar of Elon MuskAvatar of Elon Musk | **Elon Musk ** (@elonmusk) tweeted: |
| --- | --- |

> I made an offer\
> [https://sec.gov/Archives/edgar...](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0001418091/000110465922045641/tm2212748d1_sc13da.htm)

April 14, 2022[^cite_note-180]

In early 2017, Musk expressed interest in buying [Twitter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter,_Inc.) and had questioned the platform's commitment to [freedom of speech](21401843.md).[^cite_note-181][^cite_note-182][^cite_note-183] By 2022, Musk had reached 9.2% stake in the company,[^cite_note-elder-184] making him the largest shareholder.[^cite_note-Bloomberg4.14.22-185][^cite_note-187] Musk later agreed to a deal that would appoint him to Twitter's board of directors and prohibit him from acquiring more than 14.9% of the company.[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189] Days later, Musk made a \$43 billion offer to buy Twitter.[^cite_note-Bloomberg4.14.22-185][^cite_note-190] By the end of April Musk had successfully concluded his bid for approximately \$44 billion.[^cite_note-191] This included approximately \$12.5 billion in loans and \$21 billion in [equity financing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equity_\(finance\)).[^cite_note-192][^cite_note-193] Having backtracked on his initial decision,[^cite_note-194] Musk bought the company on October 27, 2022.[^cite_note-deal_closed-195]

Immediately after the acquisition, Musk fired several top Twitter executives including CEO [Parag Agrawal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parag_Agrawal);[^cite_note-deal_closed-195][^cite_note-196] Musk became the CEO instead.[^cite_note-197] Under Musk, Twitter instituted monthly subscriptions for a "[blue check](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter_verification)",[^cite_note-198][^cite_note-199][^cite_note-Binoy-2022-200] and laid off a significant portion of the company's staff.[^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202] Musk lessened content moderation and hate speech also increased on the platform after his takeover.[^cite_note-Knight-2022-203][^cite_note-independent2023-204][^cite_note-Wapo-205][^cite_note-206] In late 2022, Musk released [internal documents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter_Files) relating to Twitter's moderation of [Hunter Biden's laptop controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter_Biden_laptop_controversy) in the lead-up to the [2020 presidential election](48410011.md).[^cite_note-207] Musk also promised to step down as CEO after a Twitter poll,[^cite_note-208][^cite_note-209] and five months later, Musk stepped down as CEO and transitioned his role to [executive chairman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chair_\(officer\)) and [chief technology officer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_technology_officer) (CTO).[^cite_note-210]

Despite Musk stepping down as CEO, X continues to struggle with challenges such as viral [misinformation](2203174.md),[^cite_note-211] [hate speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hate_speech), and [antisemitism](1078.md) controversies.[^cite_note-212][^cite_note-213] Musk has been accused of [trying to silence some of his critics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter_under_Elon_Musk#Lawsuits) such as [Twitch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitch_\(service\)) streamer [Asmongold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asmongold), who criticized him during one of his streams.[^cite_note-free-speech-214] Musk has been accused of removing their accounts' [blue checkmarks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twitter_verification), which hinders visibility and is considered a form of [shadow banning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shadow_banning),[^cite_note-215][^cite_note-216] or suspending their accounts without justification.[^cite_note-217]

## Other activities

### Hyperloop

Main articles: [Hyperloop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperloop) and [Hyperloop pod competition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperloop_pod_competition)

A long white tube about 10 feet in diameter

A tube part of the 2017 Hyperloop pod competition, sponsored by SpaceX

In August 2013, Musk announced plans for a version of a [vactrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vactrain), and assigned engineers from SpaceX and Tesla to design a transport system between [Greater Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Los_Angeles) and the [San Francisco Bay Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Francisco_Bay_Area), at an estimated cost of \$6 billion.[^cite_note-218][^cite_note-219] Later that year, Musk unveiled the concept, dubbed the Hyperloop,[^cite_note-220] intended to make travel cheaper than any other [mode of transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mode_of_transport) for such long distances.[^cite_note-221]

### OpenAI and xAI

Main articles: [OpenAI](48795986.md) and [xAI (company)](74330685.md)

In December 2015, Musk co-founded OpenAI, a not-for-profit [artificial intelligence](1164.md) (AI) research company aiming to develop [artificial general intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_general_intelligence), intended to be safe and beneficial to humanity.[^cite_note-NYT_AI-222] Musk pledged \$1 billion of funding to the company,[^cite_note-223] but only donated \$50 million.[^cite_note-224] In 2018, Musk left the OpenAI board.[^cite_note-225] Since 2018, OpenAI has made significant advances in machine learning.[^cite_note-226] In July 2023, Musk launched the artificial intelligence company [xAI](74330685.md), which aims to develop a [generative AI](73291755.md) program that competes with existing offerings like OpenAI's [ChatGPT](72417803.md). Musk obtained funding from investors in SpaceX and Tesla,[^cite_note-227] and xAI hired engineers from Google and OpenAI.[^cite_note-228]

### Private jet

Main articles: [ElonJet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ElonJet) and [2022 Twitter suspensions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Twitter_suspensions)

| Avatar of Elon MuskAvatar of Elon Musk | **Elon Musk** (@elonmusk) tweeted: |
| --- | --- |

> Same doxxing rules apply to "journalists" as to everyone else

December 16, 2022[^cite_note-229]

Musk uses a private jet owned by Falcon Landing LLC, a SpaceX-linked company, and acquired a second jet in August 2020.[^cite_note-flight-230][^cite_note-231] His heavy use of the jets and the consequent [fossil fuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuel) usage have received criticism.[^cite_note-flight-230][^cite_note-232] Musk's flight usage is tracked on social media through ElonJet.[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-WP-234][^cite_note-Sky-235] In December 2022, Musk banned the ElonJet account on Twitter, and made temporary bans on the accounts of journalists that posted stories regarding the incident, including [Donie O'Sullivan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donie_O'Sullivan_\(journalist\)), [Keith Olbermann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keith_Olbermann), and journalists from *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, *[The Washington Post](102226.md)*, [CNN](62028.md), and *[The Intercept](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Intercept).[^cite_note-Taylor-2022-236]*

### Grok

This section is an excerpt from [Grok (chatbot)](75223933.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Grok_\(chatbot\)\&action=edit)]

[Grok](75223933.md) is a series of [generative AI](73291755.md) large language models developed by [SpaceXAI](74330685.md). It was launched in November 2023 by Elon Musk as an initiative based on the [large language model](73248112.md) (LLM) of the same name. Grok has apps for [iOS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IOS) and [Android](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Android_\(operating_system\)) and is integrated with the [X](9988187.md) social network and [Tesla](5533631.md)'s [Optimus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Optimus_\(robot\)) robot where it has [chatbot](148349.md) functionality. Grok is named after the verb *[to grok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grok)*, created by the American [science fiction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_fiction) author [Robert A. Heinlein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_A._Heinlein) to convey a form of deep, intuitive understanding.

SpaceXAI has released several Grok models. Grok-1 was open-sourced in 2024,[^cite_note-237] followed by Grok-2, Grok 3, and Grok 4 model families,[^cite_note-238] with Grok 4.5 released in 2026.[^cite_note-Grok_(chatbot)_\:10-239] Different versions added capabilities including image generation,[^cite_note-240] web search,[^cite_note-241] "Think" mode to simulate reasoning,[^cite_note-242], agentic coding tool called Grok Build,[^cite_note-243] and AI teammates called Grok Bot.[^cite_note-244] Musk admitted in 2026 that Grok was partially [distilled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knowledge_distillation) from [OpenAI](48795986.md)'s GPT models.[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-246] Starting with Grok 4.5, the models are co-developed with incoming SpaceXAI subsidiary [Cursor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cursor_\(company\)).[^cite_note-Grok_(chatbot)_\:10-239]

The bot has received criticism for promoting [conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conspiracy_theories), praising [Adolf Hitler](2731583.md), using [antisemitic tropes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitic_tropes), and [creating nonconsensual, sexualized images of undressed women and children](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grok_sexual_deepfake_scandal).

Grok generates articles for the [encyclopedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encyclopedia) website [Grokipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grokipedia), launched in October 2025 by Musk as an alternative to [Wikipedia](5043734.md), which he described as "[woke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woke)" and [politically biased](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ideological_bias_on_Wikipedia).

#### Grokipedia

Main article: [Grokipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grokipedia)

In October 2025, Musk's company xAI launched Grokipedia, an AI-generated online encyclopedia that he promoted as an alternative to Wikipedia.[^cite_note-rw-247][^cite_note-248] Articles on Grokipedia were generated and reviewed by xAI's [Grok](75223933.md) chatbot[^cite_note-249] until the site effectively froze in April 2026.[^cite_note-250]

Media coverage and academic analysis described Grokipedia as frequently reusing Wikipedia content but framing contested political and social topics in line with Musk's own views and right-wing narratives. A study by Cornell University researchers and NBC News stated that Grokipedia cites sources that are blacklisted or considered "generally unreliable" on Wikipedia, for example, the conspiracy site [Infowars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infowars) and the neo-Nazi forum [Stormfront](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stormfront_\(website\)). *[Wired](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wired_\(magazine\))*, *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* and *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* criticized Grokipedia for factual errors and for presenting Musk himself in unusually positive terms while downplaying controversies.[^cite_note-rw-247][^cite_note-251][^cite_note-252][^cite_note-253]

Please help [improve it](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Elon_Musk\&action=edit) by adding information on neglected viewpoints. Relevant discussion may be found on the [talk page](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk\:Elon_Musk). *(April 2026)* |
